             South Azadegan Oilfield Project

  Contract No:          AZNS-127 Re-Entry#01 Program                 Page 9 of 229 CCA/C/1401/27

1.  General Information

   1.1 Field Introduction
Azadegan South Oil Field is situated in West Karoun province on a large long-axis anticline with N-S
trending with approximate dimensions about 20 km width and 60 km length near to Iran-Iraq border,
to the east of Yaran Oil Field.
The trap area of the south Azadegan is limited to 43.2x17.2 km. The Azadegan Oil field was discovered
in 1999, when Well AZN-1 was drilled vertically down to the Cretaceous Fahliyan Formation with
total depth of 4,643m. The results of this well, revealed the reservoir characteristics of the field.
Following to the result of the well no. AZN-1, another 5 exploration wells was drilled in different parts
of this huge field down to Fahliyan Formation. In the later stage the well No.10 assumed as exploration
well for evaluation of deeper formations. This well was drilled in 2008, down to the depth of 5340m
(Neyriz Fm.), mainly to evaluate the Jurassic formations.
Based on the exploration wells results, carbonate reservoir of Sarvak Formation is broadly developed
in Azadegan Field. This formation was penetrated in all of seven exploration wells (AZN-1, AZN-2,
AZN-3, AZN-4, AZN-5, AZN-6 and AZN-10). Clastic oil-bearing zones of Kazhdumi Formation
developed in main anticline of Azadegan Field, extending from north to center of South Azadegan. The
Clastic oil-bearing zones of Gadvan Formation developed only in crest area and south parts of South
Azadegan Filed, which was discovered by AZN-1 in crest and AZN-4 in south parts. The carbonates
of Fahliyan Formation also developed in Azadegan field, but the oil reservoir of this formation
developed in South part of South Azadegan.
The Azadegan Field is an elongated anticline, trending in N-S direction, belonging to "Arabian Trend".
The stratigraphy sequence of the field includes AghaJari, Gachsaran, Fahliyan, Pabdeh, Gurpi, Ilam,
Laffan, Sarvak, Kazhdumi, Dariyan, Gadvan and Fahliyan Formations, from top to bottom. This
sequence in well no AZN-10, the deepest up to now drilled well of the field, continues with Garau,
Gotnia, Najmah, Sargalu, Alan, Mus and Neyriz formations.
The Dominant lithofacies of Sarvak Formation are Rudist-bearing packstone and partially grainstone,
which contains abundant bioclasts. Some other facies like matrix-dominated wackestone and mudstone
are also found.
The AZADEGAN Oilfield is Located in Khuzestan Province and is Approximately Located 80
Kilometers West of Ahwaz City, near to Iran-Iraq Border. It is in The Northwest of The YADAVARAN
Field and in The West of JUFEYR Field. The Average Monthly Temperature is 45ºc in summer, and
Highest Temperature is 52ºc. The Lowest Temperature is -4.4ºc in winter. Maximum Rainfall is 78 mm
in February, Winter Season.
This Document covers the work over of well AZNS-122. Main headlines of program are de-complete
the well and run new completion string.

   1.3 Area Distribution

According to the drilling history, the CONTRACT AREA is distributed into three blocks as per the
geological features with regard to DRILLING OPERATION. This distribution may differ according to
knowledge accumulated in the future WORK.
Contract area is as per below:

              X (m)                           Y (m)

                   185640                                      3434275

                   188020                                      3477361

                   205224                                      3476408

                   202842                                      3433327

                                            Table 1-2 Area Distribution

  Block I is located on the north part of the CONTRACT AREA in north of well AZN-010. Gachsaran
  formation is characterized by abnormal high pressure in this block.

  Block II is located between well AZN-010 and AZN-011. In this block, Gachsaran formation is
  characterized by pressure tending to normal

  Block III is located on the south of line well AZN-011. In this block, Gachsaran formation is
  characterized by normal pressure and all drilled wells in this block suffered from complete loss at
  Pabdeh formation.

Compressive Strength for reinforced concrete after 28 days: 280 kg/cm2

• Fuel tank concrete pond dimension: Length 20m ×Width 12m ×Height 0.5m
• Drainage: Width 30cm × Depth 30cm
• Burning pit: 15m×15m square, located on South-East or South-West with at least 350m distance from
well head
• Cellar to cellar distance in multi-well well pad: 15m
• Conductor pipe depth under cellar bottom  is 5.5m. The conductor pipe will be driven while
constructing the well pad.
• Mud material dimension is 25m*35m +granular surface
• Mud drying dimension is 25m*35m + concrete surface
• Well pad surface is gravel. Access road granular surface. The level of well pads is 3.95 MSL

1. Types of well pads:

• One well in one pad.
• Two wells in one pad. 2 Sarvak horizontal wells or 1 Sarvak horizontal well plus 1 vertical/directional
well).
• Three wells in one pad: 2 Sarvak horizontal wells and 1 vertical/directional wells.

   1.7 Drilling History

26" Hole Section

Rig moving initiated on 95.09.27 and spudded rig 304 on 95.10.08 at 1200hrs.

Commenced drilling 26” hole section from 14m to 150m while experienced low ROP and bit balled up

several times. Pulled out to surface, Ran in hole with same rock bit and 26" reamer to 150m and reamed

from surface to 150m. Ran 20” casing as following with no major problem:

 20" stab in shoe 94ppf, k-55, BTC (KTK Co.)

 2 joints 20" casing 94ppf, k-55, BTC to 25m

 Circulated mud and check float shoe-ok

 Continued run with 10 joints 20" casing 94ppf, K-55, BTC to 146m (Totally run: 12 joints 20" casing

94ppf, K-55, BTC) to 146m. Cemented 20” casing in place as below:

 Sting in, lined up cement line and tested it with 2000psi-ok.

 Pumped 20bbl fresh water as spacer, mixed and pumped 689sxs cement class "G" Kerman (slurry

weight: 118pcf, slurry volume: 170bbl), displaced with 7bbl 68pcf mud (1.7bbl short displace).

Note: Got 40bbl cement slurry and 10bbl contaminate as return on surface.

17 1/2" Hole section

Drilling of 17-1/2” hole section started on 12th Dey 1395 by new 17-1/2” rock bit and 17-1/2” string

stabilizer placed at 20m, from 150m to 1064m while pumped salt saturated water several times to

prevent balled up bit and displaced hole with 74pcf Kcl polymer mud at 502m.

Continued drilling 17-1/2” hole section by 17-1/2” from 1064m to 13-3/8” casing point at 1441m with

3-6bph loss while pumped hi-vis pill several times and pumped salt saturated water several times to

prevent balled up bit. Condition hole prior logging, pulled out to surface and ran in hole with

Sonic/Caliper/CDR/GR wire line logging tool string to 1438m (3m off bottom), took weight, tried to

pass this point-not succeed, logged up from 1438 to 1280m, due to failure of Sonic tools, stopped log

up and pulled out of hole to surface, changed sonic tools and ran in hole with same logging tool string

Continued drilling 12-1/4” hole from 1989m to 2000m while periodically pumped hi-vis. Pulled out of

at 2344.6m, engaged and catch fish, pulled out to surface while completely recovered fish.

Perforation and Completion

 Perforation

Ran in hole with 6-1/8” bit to top of cement at 4173m, circulated mud, pumped & displaced 33bbl

84pcf CaCl2 hi-vis pill (Length: 273m, from 4173m to 3900m), pulled out of hole to surface with NL-

NG.

Loaded guns, performed pre job safety meeting, ran TCP/DST string while drifted DPs and filled up

string with 79.5pcf CaCl2 as cushion to 4077m, Rigged up logging equipment and ran in hole with

CCL/GR logging tool string to 3925m, logged up to surface and correlated depth, L/D logging

equipment.

Set DST packer at 3954m, pressure tested DST packer by applying 400psi pressure in annulus for

15min-ok. Performed pre job safety meeting, N/U flow head, related lines and DST choke manifold,

applied 1200psi to annulus, opened tubing tester valve, applied 3200psi pressure through string and

step by step increased pressure to 4300psi, fired guns, released string pressure to zero, monitored tubing

pressure: zero & annulus pressure: zero psi, applied 2800psi press in annulus, opened AOR valve,

reversed circulated & got 217bbl 79.5 CaCl2 meanwhile observed no influx, continued reverse

circulation to balance mud, N/D flow head and related lines, flow check-well static, pulled out of hole

from 4077m to surface, L/D DST/TCP tools and checked TCP string, observed all bullets were fired.

To check perforated interval depth, R/U running logging equipment and ran CCL/GR logging tools to

4100m, logged up to 3700m and continued pulled out of hole to surface.

Workover Operation History:

The AZNS – 127 workover well was De-Complete by rig OEOC – 208 on Pad 54, from "08 – May –
2024" as described in the following:

Installed 8" poor boy degasser flare. Pressure test SP & function test TDS, MPs; Ok. Checked section
B: P-Seals & ring gasket; Ok. Checked section C: BTM P-Seal; Ok. Top P-Seal & ring gasket; Not Ok.
Disconnected adjustable choke of production line. L/U pump truck to XMT kill line & annulus. L/U
choke manifold to production line & annulus, meanwhile preparing 200bbl 106pcf mud. Pressure test
all line after rectify several leakages. Open Ann & monitor Press; 4700 psi. Bleed off Press to 3000 psi,
Build up to3800 psi in 1 Min. Increase Press f/ 3800 to 3900 psi in 5 Min.

P/U 60 Jts 5" DP Jt by Jt, made & R/B 20 Stds to derrick. Monitored annulus pressure build up to
4800psi after 6 hrs. Opened the annulus thru choke manifold to oil storage; after 6min. decreased
pressure to 980psi. Decreased pressure gradually to 500psi in 20min. Closed the annulus; Shut in
pressure 500psi. Built up to 1000psi in 15min. Opened the annulus, suddenly dropped to zero. After
6min closed the annulus. Opened the 2SV, top master valve & production valve. Tightened XMT cap.
Connected hand pump to exit block, tried to open TR3SV. Monitored tubing pressure 3400psi. Opened
the well f/ TBG to oil storage tank; dropped pressure to zero in 2min. Closed the well. Built up to
3700psi in 2min. L/U annulus to choke manifold. Obs. annulus pressure built up to 4300psi in 3 hrs.
Opened the well & pressure decreased to 600psi. Closed the annulus. Pressure rapidly built up to
3300psi. Opened the well again. Pressure suddenly dropped to zero. Flew the well for 10min. Closed
the well, built up to 3300psi. Monitored the well for1 hrs. Pressure built up to 4300psi.

Due to jammed stem of inside SOV of THS in close position, installed pressure gauge on annulus to
choke manifold line & pressure test same to 7000psi, Ok. R/U slickline equipment. Installed slickline
BOP & lubricator on XMT cap. Tried to pressure test, Obs. leakage between lubricator & BOP.
Changed lubricator O-ring & tried to pressure test; No-Success. L/D previous BOP. Installed another
slickline BOP & Lubricator on XMT cap. Pressure test slickline BOP & lubricator w/ 7000psi to 15min;
Ok. RIH w/ 3.4" gauge cutter to TBG HGR, tried to pass this depth; No-Success. Pulled out to surface
& R/D slickline BOP & lubricator.

Installed 4 – 1/16" 10k lubricator on XMT. Pressure test to 7000psi for 15min; Ok. RIH w/ BPV
retrieving tools to TBG HGR & retrieved 4" BPV f/ TBG HGR. L/D 4 – 1/16" 10k lubricator. Due to
unable to open TR3SV, decided M/U 5" DPs. R/D slickline equipment.

R/U slickline equipment. Pressure test; Ok. Fixed CL port exit block. Ran 3.4" gauge cutter to 206ft,
RIH w/ 3.437" protection sleeve to 190ft, opened TR3SV while holding pressure to 1200psi. Applied
7500psi pressure in CL & opened TR3SV. Cont. running tools to 200ft, took weight, tried to set
protection sleeve by jarring several times; No-Success. Flew the well with 1/4" choke size & Cont.
work on set protection sleeve; No-Success. Pulled out tools to 191ft. Obs. stuck tools. Worked on stuck
to max. 60lb over-pull; No-Success. Finally flew the well by annulus & tubing. Tools got free. Cont.
pulled out tools to 50ft & stuck tools again. R/D lubricator. Obs. cut the slickline wire from 50ft & left

────────────────────────────────────────────
             South Azadegan Oilfield Project

  Contract No:          AZNS-127 Re-Entry#01 Program                Page 52 of 229 CCA/C/1401/27

in hole about 3m tools & 13m wire. RIH w/ 2.5" LIB to 157ft, POOH to surface; Obs. wire mark on
same.

While wait on OEOC slickline service, RIH w/ 2.8" spear to TOF. Worked on fish & retrieved 2.45m
of wire after 3 attempts. RIH w/ 2.8" LIB to 157ft, POOH to surface. Obs. rope socket marked on same.
RIH w/ 2.8" blind box, POOH to surface. Rope socket marked on same. Welded 3.2" skirt on 2.2" JDC,
RIH w/ JDC to TOF. Engaged w/ same. Tried to pull out, Obs. indication of shear JDC Shear pin.
POOH to surface. Obs. sheared JDC Shear pin. Redressed w/ steel shear pin. RIH w/ JDC to TOF
again. Engaged & worked on fish, Pulled to surface. Obs. retrieved 10ft stem of fish. RIH w/ 2.8" LIB
to TOF, POOH to surface. Obs. slickline mechanical jar on the same. RIH w/ JDC to engage w/ jar.
Worked on fish, jarring up. Pulling out fish 20ft. Cont. worked on stuck. RIH w/ blind box & knock on
TOF, without progress. POOH to surface. RIH w/ JDC to engage w/ Jar. Worked on fish, jarring up.
POOH to surface. R/D slickline equipment.

In order to work on fish w/ coiled tubing, wait on receive coiled tubing unit. While waiting on OEOC
slickline service, R/U CT & M/U 2 – 1/8" dimple connector on 1 – 1/2" CT pipe & performed pull test
to max 20klb. Obs. failure in dimple connector, disconnected same. M/U 1 – 11/16" roll on connector
on 1 – 1/2" CT pipe & performed pull test to max 20klb, Ok. Installed CT riser on XMT & CT BOP.
Close CT blind rams & tried to perform pressure test; No-Success. Tightened between two risers flange
& tried to perform pressure test; No-Success. P/U riser & BOP; Tried to perform pressure test; No-
Success. Changed 1 Jt CT riser & performed pressure test CT BOP & risers, Ok. RIH CT BHA to 48m
& engaged w/ the fish; Obs. stuck the fish. Worked on stuck to max 18klb over-pull; No-Success. While
shearing JDC Shear pin @ 2 times & POOH to surface & redressed w/ brass shear pin.

In the 3rd attempt, caught the fish & worked on same upward to 18klb over-pull while bleeding off
annulus pressure f/ 4800 to 2000psi. Obs. fish got free, jumped same & sheared the JDC Shear Pin.
POOH to surface. Cut 12m 1 – 1/2" CT pipe & changed roll on connector. Performed pull test to 20klb;
Ok. RIH w/ 1 – 1/2" CT & 3.2" JDC to 48m. Worked on fish & POOH to surface. In the last attempt
caught, engaged the fish & bleed off annulus pressure f/ 4800 to 2000psi. Pulled out f/ 48 to 10m w/ 2
– 3klb drag. Obs. took over-pull. Worked on stuck upward to max 18klb; No-Success. Then Obs. CT
got free & POOH to surface. Closed swab valve & unable close TMV. Obs. sheared JDC Shear pin &
TOF located in TMV. Installed 1 No 4 – 1/16" 10k gate valve on XMT. Redressed JDC shear pin to
steel & M/U on CT BHA. Tightened all bolts. Performed pressure test to 6000psi for 10min; Ok.

Due to refused work on fish & stopped operation from OEOC CT service, wait on CT Service to make
a decision. Changed JDC shear pin to brass. RIH w/ 1 – 1/2" CT & 3.2" JDC to 11m & engaged w/ the
fish. Worked on stuck upward to max 18klb over-pull; No-Success. Slack off 6klb weight on JDC &
sheared JDC Shear Pin. POOH to surface. R/D CT injector head. R/U slickline equipment & worked
on fish w/ JDC jarring up several times; No-Success.

Performed injectivity test & monitored the well & checked communication between string & annulus;
Ok. Pumped &  direct  Circ. 30bbl fresh water by applying back pressure  in order  to check
communication between string & annulus; Ok. Commenced killing the well by W&W method,

"pumping pressure f/ 5500 to 3600psi, annulus pressure f/5500 to 2400psi". Meanwhile R/D slickline
equipment & CT raiser.

In order to work on fish closed 3SV & released above pressure, M/U & RIH with 3 – 1/2" handmade
JDC & tried to engage with fish several times, in the last attempt successfully engaged with fish &
recovered w/ max 10klb over-pull. Operate 3SV; Ok. Monitored the well pressure, observed wellhead
pressure decreased f/ 500 to 300psi. Bleed off to zero & remained constant. Annulus pressure decreased
f/ 2800 to 2000psi, bleed off to 1800psi & remained constant. Performed cavity pressure test to 4000psi
for 15min; Ok. Pumped & direct Circ. 125bbl 137pcf mud, "pumping pressure f/ 1300 – 2800psi to
1700psi & annulus pressure 2400psi". Got 78 to 85pcf gas cut mud in return. Stopped pumping.

Bleed off pressure f/ 2200psi to zero step by step & monitored the pressure for any build up in each
step; Negative. Wellhead pressure decreased to zero & remained constant. Pumped & direct Circ.
125bbl 145pcf mud. Stopped pumping. Bleed off pressure f/ 700psi to zero step by step & remained
constant. Pumped 20bbl 135pcf mud to annulus. Obs. wellhead & annulus pressure decreased to zero.
Opened cap of 3/8" CIL & monitored for 1 hr. Ok. Cut the CIL. Closed 3SV & removed XMT cap.
Installed TWCV in TBG HGR & test same to 5000psi for 15min; Ok. N/D XMT while passing CIL
thru BTM section.

N/D XMT & N/U 13 – 5/8" 10k BOP stack. Connected hydraulic, choke & kill lines. L/U cement line
to BOP & performed BOP pressure test as NIOC policy; Ok. Passed CL f/ BOP stack to rig floor.
Installed bell nipple. Changed annulus to C/M line, tested same; Ok. P/U & M/U 5" DP, XOS (4 – 1/2"
IF * 4 – 1/2" TPCQ), 4 – 1/2" TBG HGR running tools. Retrieved TWCV. Screw TBG HGR running
tools + X.O.S (4 – 1/2" TPCQ * 4 – 1/2" IF) + 1 Jt 5" DP + 1 No. Pup Jt on TBG HGR. Performed
pressure test of running tool O-ring to 1000psi, Ok. F/U string, opened 3SV & monitored the well, Ok.
Back off running tools & R/D same. Close 3SV. R/U Golpa flange, BOP & lubricator of slickline
(OEOC Co.), performed pressure test same w/ 1000psi. Obs. leakage between Golpa flange & 4 –
1/16"gate valve. R/D 4 – 1/16" gate valve, changed ring gasket, pressure tested same again to 1000psi,
Ok. Ran 3.4" gauge ring to 61m (3SV depth), Obs. drag @59m, pulled out to surface. Ran spear to 61m
(3SV depth) to catch possible cable in the well, pulled out to surface, Obs. No cable. Ran w/ protection
sleeve to 61m (3SVdepth), held 7500psi pressure inside CL, set protection sleeve inside 3SV, retrieved
protection sleeve & pulled out to surface. B/O slickline BOP. R/U 4 – 1/16" gate valve on Golpa flange,
M/U slickline BOP & lubricator & performed pressure test same w/1000psi, Ok.

Ran w/ protection sleeve to 61m, held 7500psi pressure inside CL, set protection sleeve inside 3SV &
pulled out to surface. Ran w/ 2.230" gauge cutter to 61m (3SV depth), took weight, tried to pull out,
Obs. slickline string stuck, tried to pass this point for several times, No Success. Pumped 5bbl 138pcf
mud wile working on stuck, No success. Increased CL pressure to 10,000psi step by step, Obs. slickline
string got free. Pulled out gauge cutter to surface & L/D same. Ran with GS pulling tools of protection
sleeve & retrieved same. Pulled out protection sleeve to surface. Ran back in hole w/ protection sleeve
to 61m (3SV depth) & tried to set protection sleeve for several times, No Success. POOH separation
sleeve to surface. Decided to extend protection sleeve, RIH w/ new protection sleeve to 61m, set

protection sleeve inside 3SV. RIH w/ 1 – 7/8" stem to 80m, checked opening of 3SV flapper, Ok,

slickline cable tension decided to POOH to surface. B/O & L/D same.

R/D slickline BOP, lubricator, upper & lower sheaves. Installed X.O.S (4 – 1/16" flange * 4 – 1/2" IF
box) on gate valve, pressure tested same w/ 1000psi, Ok. R/D mouse hole, R/U wireline equipment,

& cut box connection, welded 7" new Vam box connection on top of 4 – 1/16" flange & installed same

Held PJSM Assembled & armed colliding tools, RIH to 3850m, spaced out to 3845m, performed
colliding, POOH colliding tools to surface, Obs. running tools of colliding tools & CCL left in hole.
Pumped 115bbl 137pcf mud, Cont. pumping 685bbl 106pcf mud while dumping return mud, after

120klb overpull & applied 5klb.ft torque as forward for several times, No Success. B/O & L/D 2 Jts
DP, X.O.S (4 – 1/2" TPCQ * 4 – 1/2" IF) & TBG HGR running tools.

P/U & M/U TBG HGR running tools, landing Jt, Gol. Flange, 4 – 1/16" G.Flange & X.O.S (4 – 1/16"
flange * 7" N. Vam), performed break Circ., NL-NG. Obs. the well, Static. R/U wireline equipment
(Sealand Co.) & performed pressure test of wireline BOP w/ 1000psi, Ok. RIH w/ CCL & sinker bar
to 3786m, took weight, tried to pass this point for several times, No Success. POOH to surface, changed
CCL, RIH w/ 1 – 7/8" CCL & sinker bar to 3786m, took weight, tried to pass this point for several
times, No Success. POOH to surface. L/D wireline equipment. L/D 4 – 1/16" X.O.S (4 – 1/16" flange
* 7" N. Vam), 4 – 1/16" gate valve, Gol. Flange, landing Jt & TBG HGR running tools, closed shear
rams & performed break circulation, NL-NG. M/U TBG HGR running tools, X.O.S (4 – 1/12" TPCQ
* 4 – 1/2" IF), 2 Jts 5" DP as landing Jt & screw on TBG HGR, at the beginning of P/U string, Obs.
270klb on weight indicator, while pulling the string, indication of EXP.Jt movement not observed
(expansion joint is in full open position). Due to condition of EXP.Jt unable to follow releasing
mechanism of Anchor seal due to TBG HGR is inside of THS unable to rotate.

P/U string 30cm to be sure TBG HGR is not inside THS (weight: 290klb), set torque limit on 7klb.ft,

several times, No Success. Released the TQ, P/U string to 400klb on weight indicator, to release Anchor

coupling protectors parted because of eroded condition. After Jt No. 35 some of cross couplings had
been lost, eroded & parted. Obs. some of asphaltene on cross coupling protectors of injection line &
slips area of 4 – 1/2" TBGs so need to be washed with steam, which lead delay in POOH completion
string.

P/U & M/U 7 – 7/8" Over shot (w/ 4 – 3/4" Basket Grapple Mill Control, 4 – 1/2" Basket Grapple) &
Fishing BHA & RIH to 1880m, NL-NG. Estimated TOF: 2706m. Took weight @ 1880m, tried to pass
several times; No Success. Established Circ. & pass gently. Cont. wash down to 2660m while trying to
pass several intervals w/o wash down; No Success (Asphaltene precipitation indication); NL-NG.
Meanwhile got 150bbl 50pcf gas cut mud & dumped same @ 2020m. Established Circ. to uniform Mw
in/out on 106pcf, got 300bbl 75 – 95pcf gas cut mud w/ NL-NG. Obs. huge amount of asphaltene
precipitation on shakers. Resumed wash down to 2731m while took weight several depth & pass same.
Confirmed top of fish @2731m. Tried to catch the fish several times; No Success. Commenced POOH
w/ 7 – 7/8" over shot & fishing BHA f/2731m to surface. Circ. CWH; NL-NG. Obs. some scratches on
outer body & edge of over shot guide.

R/U slickline equipment & ran 3.8" magnet to 2731m, held up. POOH to surface. 1lb junk recovered
(mixed with asphaltene). R/D slickline equipment while waiting on receive 8 – 3/8" Mill. P/U & M/U
12 Jts 6 – 1/2" DC, 3 Jts 5" HWDP & RIH OEDPs to 1250m Jt by Jt. Circ. CWH; NL-NG. POOH
string to surface & L/D same. RIH OEDPs by 12 Jts 4 – 3/4" DC & 12 Jts 3 – 1/2" DP. POOH OEDPs
to surface & R/B. While Circ. CWH; NL-NG. Difficulty B/O & L/D 1 Jt 5" Pup Jt, 5" FOSV & Circ.

No Success. Set 11" wear bushing in THS.

window & continue drilling directionally to TD @4226m.

     Formation Sampling

 Sample would be taken from Gachsaran to Gadvan every 2-5 meters

 NOTE: In addition to this general rule, geologist may decide to take more samples.

     1. RIH w/ 8 – 1/2" bit & 9 – 5/8" scraper, scrap 9 – 5/8" casing prior to Corrosion log, POOH
        to surface.
     2. Change ram, install shooting nipple, R/U wireline logging equipment.
     3. RIH w/ Corrosion log to check 9 – 5/8" casing, POOH to surface & R/D logging equipment,
      change ram.
     4. R/U & run 5" & 3 – 1/2" open end DPs to 3390mMD, set 40bbl cement as balance plug, POOH.
     5. RIH 8 – 1/2" bit & dress cement to 3350mMD. Perform positive pressure test CMT POOH.

 NOTE:
     If pressure test is not Ok, follow contingency plan #6.

     6. RIH w/ 8 – 1/2" bit & 9 – 5/8" scraper, scrap whipstock setting depth & displace mud in
       hole wby 85pcf mud, observe the well, POOH.
     7. M/U & RIH w/ 9 – 5/8" permanent hydraulic set whipstock (suitable for 9 – 5/8" casing
        53.5ppf), set same & open window @ 3337m.
     8. Drill 3m in formation to 3340m, POOH to surface.
     9. P/U & M/U new 8 – 1/2" rock bit & directional BHA, RIH to bottom.
    10. Continue drilling 8 – 1/2" f/ 3340 to 3496m.
    11. Perform hole condition, POOH to surface.
    12. P/U & M/U new 8 – 1/2" rock bit & directional BHA, RIH to bottom.
    13. Continue drilling 8 – 1/2" hole to 3652m (DOP).
    14. Perform hole condition, POOH to surface to change BHA.
    15. P/U & M/U new 8 – 1/2" PDC bit & BHA, RIH to bottom.
    16. Continue directional drilling 8 – 1/2" hole f/ 3652 to section TD @3939m.
    17. CBU, perform condition trip to last casing shoe prior to logging, RIH to bottom, CBU,
       spot pill.
    18. POOH to surface & L/D PDC bit.
    19. R/U casing running gear, run 7" liner to bottom, R/D casing running gear.
    20. Perform 7" liner cementing operation.
    21. POOH w/ liner hanger setting tool to 2800m, flush string & CBU.
    22. POOH to surface & L/D liner hanger setting tool.
    23. P/U & M/U 8 – 1/2" MT bit BHA, RIH to liner lap @ ±3150m while D/O cement, Circulate
    & clean the well, POOH.

 NOTE:

 6.  Directional Drilling Operation

 6.1    Hydraulic Set Whipstock System Operation Procedure

  SCOPE

  The following procedures are intended to provide concise but definitive steps to follow in
   operations involving the milling of  lateral hole sections from a parent well using the
   Hydraulic Set Whipstock System.

  The Hydraulic Set Whipstock System  is a hydraulically set whipstock  that requires a
   hydraulic force to activate the packer assembly, setting itself in the casing.

   Milling a window in casing using a whipstock usually consists of one run for setting the
  Whipstock in place and milling and completing the window through the casing wall and into
   the formation, while dressing the window to eliminate drag.

  EQUIPMENT REQUIRED

  Hole Preparation Run Equipment

        • A Full Gauge Drift Mill
        •  Casing Scrapper
        •  Cross Over Sub
        •  String Stabilizer
        • Pony Drill Collar or 1 Joint of Drill collar
        •  String Stabilizer
        •  Drill Collars
        •  Drilling Jar
        •  Drill Collars
        • HWDP’s
        •  Balance Of Drill Pipes To Surface

  The stiffness of this assembly will serve to identify any problem doglegs or restrictions
   in the hole that may hamper running of the whipstock assembly.

  Whipstock Run Equipment

        •  Hydraulic Set Whipstock
        •  Tri-Mill (Optional Bi-Mill or a combination of Rugby/Back/Window Mill)
        •  Running Tools
        •  Nuzzle plug Assembly

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
             South Azadegan Oilfield Project

 Contract No:          AZNS-127 Re-Entry#01 Program                Page 76 of 229CCA/C/1401/27

        •  Flex Joint (1 joint of HWDP)
        •  Universal Bottom Hole Orientation Sub (UBHO sub) or MWD
        • Pup joints (for space-out if necessary)
        •  Tool kit

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
             South Azadegan Oilfield Project

 Contract No:          AZNS-127 Re-Entry#01 Program                Page 77 of 229CCA/C/1401/27

  PRE-OPERATIONAL PROCEDURES
  Pre-Run Checklist
  As part of the Pre-operational Procedures, all whipstock jobs should include a  completed
  Pre-Run Preparation Checklist.

                                 Pre‐Run Preparation
                                             Checklist

  † The Packer slip is in place, is in good condition, and is the correct size for the casing size and weight  and the
    Allen screws are tight.

  † The shear pins are properly installed and in good condition and the Number of Shear pins are enough.

   † The shear screw is the proper size, and it will make up into the Whipface.

   † The shear screw will fit through the Rugby/Lead Mill hole.

  † Running Tools, Nuzzle plug Assembly and Hydraulic Lines and Fittings are in good condition and properly installed.

   † All mills are on location and the OD’s are correct for the casing’s size/weight. (Check mill OD’s with a gauge ring that
     is OD + 1/32").

   † All downhole equipment has been properly measured and OD’s, ID’s, and lengths have been properly recorded.

   † The slots on the gyro match and are compatible with the lug assembly on the orientation sub.

   † The survey tools will pass through all drill string tool joints and drill collar ID’s.

  †  All rotary connections are compatible.

   † All rotary shouldered connections are made up to the recommended torque.

  †  Enough Pump Pressure is available on Site for setting the Packer.

  Making the Hole Preparation Run

   1.    Make a run to ensure that the casing is clean and there is smooth and clean place for
           setting the packer. The run should include the following (from the bottom):

        • A Full Gauge Drift Mill
        •  Casing Scrapper (Do not run casing scrapers across open perforations.)
        •  Cross Over Sub (Bit Sub with Float)
        •  String Stabilizer
        • Pony Drill Collar or 1 Joint of Drill collar
        •  String Stabilizer
        •  Drill Collars
        •  Drilling Jar
        •  Drill Collars
        • HWDP’s
        •  Balance of Drill Pipes To Surface

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
             South Azadegan Oilfield Project

 Contract No:          AZNS-127 Re-Entry#01 Program                Page 78 of 229CCA/C/1401/27

   2.    Run this assembly in the well to the Packer setting depth.
   3.     Tally the pipe while running in or out of the well to get an accurate measurement to
          the Packer setting depth.
   4.     Record the measurements to ensure that:
            •  the whipstock can be run and set at the desired depth
            •  the depth measurement to the Packer setting depth is accurate
            •  the casing wall is clean where the whipstock is to be set

  NOTE: Scrape Packer setting depth twice.

   Note: It is very important to get an accurate measurement for the packer setting depth so the
   Whipstock will be oriented as to setting depth as possible. Using this measurement facilitates
   proper placement of the Whipstock.

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
             South Azadegan Oilfield Project

 Contract No:          AZNS-127 Re-Entry#01 Program                Page 79 of 229CCA/C/1401/27

   Test the MWD tool
   prior, to running the assembly in hole.

   1.  Pick up the following assembly:
     o Blank Sub or Kelly Valve (valve to be closed and run pin up)
     o Crossover Sub
     o One joint of S-135 Drill Pipe
     o Flow Through Valve
     o One joint of S-135 Drill Pipe
     o 6 joints of HWDP
     o MWD
     o One joint of S-135 Drill Pipe
     o Crossover Sub
     o Kelly or Top Drive
   2. RIH to top of Drill Pipe. With top drive or Kelly connected, record the flow rate required
       to obtain a survey. The Flow Through Valve will remain open.

────────────────────────────────────────────
             South Azadegan Oilfield Project

 Preparing the Milling Equipment

  1. Lay the whipstock on the catwalk, face up, with the top of the whipstock pointing toward the
  rig.

 WARNING
 Never use a nylon sling to lift or move a Whipstock because nylon may be cut. Always us a
  wire sling or wire rope to lift or move a Whipstock.

  2.  Complete the whipstock assembly, checking all OD’s, ID’s, and descriptions.

  3.  Pick up a joint of S-135 Drill Pipe (FLEXJOINT) and make up the Flow Through Valve to
  the top of the drill pipe and make up the Mill Assembly covered with Running Tool to the
 bottom of Drill Pipe.

  Note: If the window is exiting through two or more strings of casing or into a known hard
  formation, exclude the Watermelon Mills (in the case of using combination mills), using only the
 Rugby Mill below the single joint of DP. Depending on the casing size and grade, more than
 one Rugby Mill may be required to make multiple exits.

 Once the casing string(s) has been exited into the formation, pull out of the hole. Make up the
 Back Mill (and Window Mill) in the assembly as described above and run in the hole, reaming
 through the window with the Watermelon Mills.

  4. Make up one joint of S-135 Drill Pipe (with cone type DP strainer installed) to the top of
 Flow Through Valve and make Up MWD to the top of Drill Pipe. (or Make up UBHO Sub to
  the top of Flow Through Valve).

  5.  If a bit float have to be used, place the bit float sub above the Flow Through Valve and
  beneath the UBHO sub.

  6. Make up all connections to the recommended torque.

  7.  Ensure that everything is properly made up before continuing (see Figure).

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
             South Azadegan Oilfield Project

 Contract No:          AZNS-127 Re-Entry#01 Program                Page 81 of 229CCA/C/1401/27

   There  is a hole in a one of Rugby/Lead Mill blades for screwing the shear screw and
       attaching Rugby Mill to the Whipface. Mark the opposite blade of the blade with shear

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
             South Azadegan Oilfield Project

 Contract No:          AZNS-127 Re-Entry#01 Program                Page 82 of 229CCA/C/1401/27

      screw hole on the Rugby Mill and scribe a line from this blade, up the Back Mill (and
     Window Mill), Bit Sub, and the single joint of HWDP to the top of the UBHO Sub.

   9.  Have the survey company representative back out the set screws that hold the internal key
       in the UBHO Sub, and rotate the key until it is aligned with the scribed line. After it is in
       position, tighten the setscrews to lock the key in alignment with the scribed line.

   10. Run the Mill assembly through the BOP stack and into the casing liner/hanger (being
       careful not to damage the wear bushings or strike any closed BOP rams) to ensure that the
      whipstock’s OD  is compatible with the  drift diameter of the casing. Remove wear
      bushing if necessary.

   11. Pull the assembly out of hole.

   12. Secure a safety clamp around the whipstock body about halfway down the face of the
      Whipface while the whipstock is lying on the pipe deck/catwalk.

   13. Pick up the Whipstock with tugger from the catwalk. A whip handling device is provided
     which is bolted onto the back of whipstock. This is to be used as a false rotary to support
       the assembly in the Rotary Table when making up the mill to whip via the shear screw.
       Assist with a crane as necessary.

   14. Set Whipstock in the rotary table (rams shut).

   15. Lower the whipstock into the mouse hole (or other suitable work hole on the drill floor),
      allowing both the safety clamp and the winch/tugger line to support the whipstock’s weight.

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
             South Azadegan Oilfield Project

 Contract No:          AZNS-127 Re-Entry#01 Program                Page 83 of 229CCA/C/1401/27

   Note: Larger Whipstocks (9⅝ to 20 inches) are usually too big for the mouse hole and must
  be set in the rotary to attach to the Mill assembly. Properly sized rotary slips must be used. The
  13⅜” whipstock will not fit through the standard rotary (10⅝" ID) and the rotary bushing, in
   this event must be pulled. Use of an API bowl and slips to hold the whipstock in
   the rotary is required.

                             CAUTION
           •  Close the BOP Shear/Blind ram when working on Whipstock.
           • Do not drop anything in the wellbore when working over the wellbore.
           •  Use extreme caution when handling the whipstock, setting drill collar slips, safety
            clamps, etc. to prevent dropping the whipstock in the hole.
           •  Run the draw works in low. Pick up and set down the whipstock easily in the slips
             to prevent damage or premature setting.
           •  Ensure the lead tongs have most of the slack out of the snub line to prevent
            jerking against the back-up tongs.

   16. Put a light amount of thread compound on the shear screw, adjust Rugby Mill hole and
      Whipface and connect the whipstock assembly to the mill assembly with shear screw.

   Shear screws with a higher value should be considered when:

        •  running the whipstock in a highly deviated hole
        •  heavy mud is in the well
        •  other wellbore restrictions such as a liner hanger may be encountered

   Shear screws with a lower shear value should be considered when:

        •  whipstock is being set shallow (less than 1500 m)
        •  running and setting Whipstock with coiled tubing

   17. Attach the Hydraulic Line to the connection on the bottom of the Milling Assembly and
      back of whipstock.

   18. Fill Whipstock and Mill Assembly / Running Tool with Hydraulic Oil, allow air to bleed
       out, fit piston ensuring the top of piston is below the top running tool box connection.

   19. Run the Whipstock & Mill Assembly below the rotary table to the top of UBHO sub,
      Break the connection and top-up with Hydraulic Oil.

   20. Pick up the Milling / whipstock assembly.

   21. Install the rotary table cover.

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
             South Azadegan Oilfield Project

 Contract No:          AZNS-127 Re-Entry#01 Program                Page 84 of 229CCA/C/1401/27

   22. Remove the safety clamp and whip handling device which is bolted onto the back of
      whipstock.

   23. Remove the rotary table cover.

   24. Lower the whipstock slowly into the well, setting the rotary slips on the DP.

   25. Check the orientation of the key in the UBHO sub, make any adjustments necessary, and
       tighten the setscrews again.

   Note: For safety purposes, install a drill pipe wiper to prevent dropping the Allen wrench in the hole.

                             CAUTION
    Run the draw works in low. Pick up and set down the whipstock gently in the slips to prevent
    damage or premature setting. Make sure the lead tongs have most of the slack out of the snub
     line to prevent jerking against the back-up tongs.

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
             South Azadegan Oilfield Project

 Contract No:          AZNS-127 Re-Entry#01 Program                Page 85 of 229CCA/C/1401/27

  RUNNING PROCEDURE

  Running and Orienting the Whipstock

   1. Lower the whipstock assembly slowly into the wellbore and do not pump anything in the
      well to prevent premature setting of the whipstock.

      Do not alter the orientation of the UBHO sub to the Whipface's face when making
       the first connection. Do not let the UBHO sub turn or move, or the direction of the
                               milled window could be affected.

   2.  Refer to dogleg checklist for doglegs greater than 4 degrees. Watch the weight indicator
       carefully and record up-weights regularly.
   3.  Monitor trip-tank for proper fill up.
   4. Run the whipstock slowly, like a packer assembly, at a rate of 2 to 3 minutes per stand.
   5. Do not turn string.
   6. Do not Pump in the Well (unless an open Flow Throgh Valve is Run above the Running
       Tools).
   7.  Fill the drill string every 5 strands.

      Proceed slowly and with caution while running the whipstock assembly through the
      BOPs, wellhead, liner top, and any other restrictive sections to prevent damage or
       premature setting. The difference between the whipstock OD and the casing ID is
        minimal, making it similar to a piston, so lower the assembly very slowly in these
                     small bypass areas. DO NOT PUMP IN THE WELL.
   8. Run slowly into the hole until approximately 30 feet off the bottom.

   9.  Slowly move the whipstock assembly up and down from 5 to 20 feet to remove all
      trapped torque from the drill string.
   10. Record up and down free weights.

   11. Run survey tools on wireline to the UBHO sub if using a survey.
   12. Take survey readings
   13. To orient the whipstock, rotate the drill pipe to the right in ¼ turn increments (no more that
  ½ turn at a time) until the whipstock assembly is oriented in the correct position.
   14. Take another survey, making additional corrections as needed and lowering 4 to 5 feet after
   each increment.

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
             South Azadegan Oilfield Project

 Contract No:          AZNS-127 Re-Entry#01 Program                Page 86 of 229CCA/C/1401/27

                              CAUTION
   A crooked hole may require several readings and corrections to remove all of the
    trapped torque from the drill string. Work slowly and carefully in order to achieve
    proper orientation.

                              CAUTION
    This Model of Whipstock, because it is hinged, must be set within 30° on either
    side of the low side to prevent the whipstock’s whipface from falling across the
    wellbore.

   15. Continue taking readings until 3 sequential reading are within a 5° range before assuming
   the direction is correct.
   16. After reaching and verifying the proper direction, call a meeting with the customer, driller,
  and survey/MWD company representative to review the orientation of the whipstock’s Whipface
  once more.
   17. Continue the procedure if everyone is in agreement.
   18. POOH the survey tools and make a mark on the rotary table and the Drill Pipe, make up the
   Kelly (or Top Drive), DO NOT ALTER THE ORIENTATION WHILE MAKING UP THE
  KELLY.
   19. Lower the whipstock assembly slowly to the setting depth.
   20. Start pump and increase pressure until the packer shear pins are sheared and the packer sets
   in place (at 1200-1400 psi), hold pressure for 10 minutes to confirm the setting).

   Note: In case of Using MWD and Flow Through Valve, increase pump rate until the Flow
  Through Valve shear pins are sheared and the valve closes, then continue pumping until
   the packer shear pins are sheared and the packer sets in place.

   21. Slack off 10-15klbs and over pull 10-15k lbs to prove packer is set.
   22. Pick-up 35,000-55,000 lbs (Depend on whipstock size) over pull, continue over pull on the
   screw as required to initiate the shear. A sharp drop in the pipe occurs when the shear screw
   shears.
   23. Raise the drill string approximately 1 to 2 feet.
   24. Set the drill string back down to make sure the shear screw has sheared. Repeat if necessary.
   25. Mark the depth on the pipe each time. It should read the same each time.

   26. Set the pipe back down with approximately 20,000 lb of pressure to ensure that the Whipface
    is over against the casing wall.

   27. Raise the drill string so that the Rugby Mill is 5 to 10 feet above the top of the whipstock.

   28. Lay down the necessary drill pipe to pick up the kelly.

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
             South Azadegan Oilfield Project

 Contract No:          AZNS-127 Re-Entry#01 Program                Page 87 of 229CCA/C/1401/27

   29. Start pump with maximum allowable GPM (According to the standpipe pressure). Engage
   with Whipface with Reduced RPM (20-30). Monitor pressure drop due to breaking Nozzle
   plugs.

   Note: Do not Mill and work on Nozzle plugs more than One min with low GPM which
   leads to Mill burning.

   30. Displace the Mud in the well (if needed) and check mud properties for proper rheology for
   steel removal.

   31. The milling operation can now begin.

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
             South Azadegan Oilfield Project

 Contract No:          AZNS-127 Re-Entry#01 Program                Page 88 of 229CCA/C/1401/27

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
             South Azadegan Oilfield Project

 Contract No:          AZNS-127 Re-Entry#01 Program                Page 89 of 229CCA/C/1401/27

  Running Milling Assembly and Opening the Window

   This section assumes that the Rugby Mill has been sheared from the whipstock and raised 5
   to 10 feet above the whipstock.

  NOTE: Install Ditch Magnets

   1. Make up the kelly to the drill string.

   2.  Record string weight up and down.

   3.  Engage the pumps, slowly increasing to the planned circulation rate, and record pump
      pressure and rate.

   4.  Engage the rotary and slowly increase the speed between 20 and 40 RPM.

   5.  After the rotary has stabilized, slowly increase between 50 and 75 RPM (depending on
       the mill size).

   6.  Record the following information:
     •  Free rotary torque
     •  Pump pressure
     •  Pump rate
     •  Free weight – up and down (string weight with zero weight on mill)

   7.  While rotating, slowly lower the drill string until the Rugby Mill contacts the whipstock
      and the casing wall, causing an immediate and substantial increase in rotary torque.

   8. Mark the kelly. This is the starting point for the Rugby Mill run.

   9.  After making the initial mark on the kelly, raise the Mill 3 to 5 feet.

   10. While rotating, slowly lower the drill string again to see if the increase in rotary torque is
       indicated at the same place. Work slowly and carefully; repeat if necessary.

   11. After marking the kelly with the correct starting point for the Mill, begin milling, using
       light weights on the Mill (1,000 to 3,000 lb depending on the size of the mill and grade of
       the casing). As the window milling process begins, an increase in torque and decrease in
      weight will be indicated.

   12. As the torque increases, increase the rotary speed to the recommended RPM (based on the
       mill size).

   13. Continue milling with a constant rotary torque and weight (1,000 to 3,000 lb) on the mill.
      Additional weight may be required if the rate of penetration (ROP) begins to slow.

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
             South Azadegan Oilfield Project

 Contract No:          AZNS-127 Re-Entry#01 Program                Page 90 of 229CCA/C/1401/27

  Use recommended parameters as follows.

          Casing Size (in)         Weight on Mill (klb)            Milling RPM

             9 ⅝”                       2-15                     60-120

               7”                        2-12                     60-120

               4-1/2”                        1-7                      60-120

   14. Monitor returns for cuttings during milling operations and pump high viscosity sweeps
      as needed to remove cuttings from the well. This practice is especially important if the
     window is being milled in a lateral section.

   15. The Window Mill run is completed after milling down the Whipface’s face into the
      formation AND when the Dress Mill is centered at the bottom of the window.

   16. Ream back and forth through the window until no signs of additional torque or drag are
       indicated.

   17. Stop the rotary and slide the Mill assembly through the window several times. If any
      weight or drag is indicated, start the rotary and ream back and forth through the window
       until weight or drag is eliminated.

   18. Stop the rotary and check for weight or drag again. Repeat this process as necessary.

   19. Once the window is complete, stop the rotary, clean the hole of cuttings with high
       viscosity sweeps, and pull out of the hole without rotating.

   20. Closely inspect and gauge all mills for wear using a gauge ring of OD + 1/32"

   21. Determine the estimated size of the window.

   22. Review the window requirements with the customer to determine if the Elongation Run is
       required.

   Note: The Elongation run is recommended if:

     •  You have run a bi-Mill Assembly for opening the Window and:
     •  The customer requests it.
     • PDC bit will be used through the window for additional drilling
     •  Drill collars and stabilizers will be used through the window for additional drilling
     •  Liner will be installed through the window
     •  Double bent sub and PDM assembly will be used through the window for additional
           drilling
     •  Unusual wear is indicated on the Rugby Mill, resulting in an under-gauge hole

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
             South Azadegan Oilfield Project

 Contract No:          AZNS-127 Re-Entry#01 Program                Page 91 of 229CCA/C/1401/27

                              CAUTION
   When using a 3° whipface whipstock, do not run the Elongation assembly until
   AFTER the casing is fully exited. Otherwise, excessive torque will be encountered
    as the two Watermelon Mills begin elongating the top of the window.

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
             South Azadegan Oilfield Project

 Contract No:          AZNS-127 Re-Entry#01 Program                Page 92 of 229CCA/C/1401/27

  Elongation run

   1.  Assemble the Elongation run components
      • Balance of drill string
      • One or more drill collars or heavy weight
      • Bit Sub (minimum length, bored for float assembly)
      • Crossover sub (double pin, if Bit sub is used)
      • Tri-Mill

   Note: Be sure to eliminate the one joint of drill pipe above the Bit Sub and replace it with one
   or more drill collars/heavy weight to provide “stiffness” to the milling assembly.

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
             South Azadegan Oilfield Project

 Contract No:          AZNS-127 Re-Entry#01 Program                Page 93 of 229CCA/C/1401/27

   2. Lower the Elongation run assembly into the hole, stopping 5 to 10 feet above the
      whipstock.

   3. Make up the kelly to the drill string.

   4.  Engage the pumps and slowly increase to the desired circulation rate.

   5.  Engage the rotary and slowly increase to between 50 and 75 RPM (depending on the mill
       size).

   6.  Record the following information:
     •  Free rotary torque
     •  Free weight – up and down (string weight with zero weight on mill)
     •  Pump pressure
     •  Pump rate

   7.  While rotating, slowly lower the drill pipe string and ream back into the window.

   8. As the torque increases, increase the rotary speed to the recommended RPM (based on the
       mill size).

   9.  Continue milling with a constant rotary torque and weight on the mill.

   10. Monitor returns for cuttings during milling operations. Pump high viscosity sweeps as
      needed to remove cuttings from the well.

   11. Continue milling down the Whipface, elongating the milled window at the top with the
      Dress Mill.

   12. The Elongation run is complete (unless directed otherwise by the customer) when the
      Dress Mill is centered at the bottom of the window.

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
             South Azadegan Oilfield Project

 Contract No:          AZNS-127 Re-Entry#01 Program                Page 94 of 229CCA/C/1401/27

   13. While rotating, ream back and forth through the window until no additional weight or
      drag is indicated.

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
             South Azadegan Oilfield Project

 Contract No:          AZNS-127 Re-Entry#01 Program                Page 95 of 229CCA/C/1401/27

   Note: If using stabilizers for additional drilling through the window, it is recommended to
   drill enough hole into the formation to accommodate the stabilizers. The additional rathole
   should allow the stabilizers to be in the formation BEFORE drilling commences. Rotating
   the stabilizers or drill bits while on the Whipface can damage the Whipface and the
  window.

   14. Stop the rotary and slide the Mill assembly through the window several times. If any
      weight or drag is indicated, start the rotary and ream back and forth through the window
       until weight or drag is eliminated.

   15. Circulate the hole clean with one or more high viscosity sweeps to remove all cuttings
      from the well.

   16. When circulation is complete, stop the rotary and pull out of the hole without rotating.

   17. Inspect and gauge all mills using a gauge ring of OD + 1/32", to determine wear to the
       mill and to estimate window/hole size. Review signs of wear with the customer.  Repeat
     Run #3 if the hole appears to be undersized.

   18. Lay down the Elongation Run assembly.

 Abnormal Conditions

              Whipstock stuck

                •  Try to release the whipstock with 20000klb overpull in 7" hole and

               30000klb over pull in 9-5/8" hole.

                •   Close an annular preventor hydrill and apply 500-1000 psi pressure in

                annulus to remove casing collapse.

                •  Apply weight on bit.

                •    If no success, shear screw and pull out the whipstock out of hole with

                  fishing tool later.

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
             South Azadegan Oilfield Project

 Contract No:          AZNS-127 Re-Entry#01 Program                Page 96 of 229CCA/C/1401/27

            No success in passing the whipstock through liner lab

                •  Come back to neutral point and turn string 1/4 round in clockwise direction
               and try to pass through liner lab again.

             The packer does not set with designed setting pressure

                •  Release the pressure and apply the pressure again.

                •  Pull out of hole to surface and check the tools.

               Pressure drop during the milling operation

                •  Twisting off the string or mill:

                •  In this case the torque will be constant and progress will be stop.

                •  Pressure drop due to string washing out:

                •  In this case the standpipe pressure will decrease gradually.

                •  Pressure drop due to breaking the nozzle plugs:

                •   In  this case the pressure decreases suddenly and no change  in  other

                parameters will happen.

                •  Pressure drop due to washing out the nozzles:

                •  In this case the standpipe pressure will decrease gradually.

              Note: In all the above items pulling the string out of hole to surface
            and checking the tools will be necessary.

            No progress during milling

                •  Change  the  drilling  parameters,  for  example  increase  or  decrease

            RPM, increase WOB, decrease GPM.

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
             South Azadegan Oilfield Project

 Contract No:          AZNS-127 Re-Entry#01 Program                Page 97 of 229CCA/C/1401/27

                •    If you observe no progress in 2 hours, pulling the string out of hole to

                 surface will be necessary.

              Observing complete loss during milling

                •  Don’t continue milling till curing the complete loss.

                •    If the client insists in continuing the operation, due to working with low

            GPM, stop the milling operation for 30 minutes after each hour of milling to

                 cool the mill and prevent from thermal cracking.

          POH string due to failure of whipstock:

                •  Do not shock the string while put and pick up slips

                •  Do not shock the string while wrenching

                •  Ensure that the rotary table is locked for prevent of rotating string

                •  Pick up each stand in 5 to 7 minutes

                •  Disconnect the connections with wrench and air wrench.

                •  Be careful for 5” and 7” whipstocks , because it will has more swing in

                  larger casing size

 Caution: do not use rotary table for disconnect the connections

    8.2 8 – ½” Hole Section (3337 – 3939m)

 Challenges

       Hole cleaning
       Possible formation losses
       Tight Hole and Hole stability
       Hole instability issues
       Wellbore losses.
       Shale

 Recommendation

        It is mandatory to monitor closely the rheological properties in order to ensure adequate
         hole cleaning, run Hydraulic calculation to ensure that mud carrying capacity is enough
         to meet this critical goal.

       To combat drilled solids, the shakers should be run with the finest screens possible in
        accordance with the flow rate and minimize fluid losses from the shakers by checking
        deck angle and running appropriate screens at all times. And the mud cleaner should be
        run with the finest mesh screens, making sure that the cones are always clean and
         discharging heavy liquid into the screen, check the working pressure in order to have the
      mud cleaner working at the maximum efficiency. The centrifuge should be run as required
         to maintain the mud weight.

       Use High Pressure Washer Equipment to wash and clean the shale shaker.

        Pre-treat mud received from previous section with Sodium Bicarbonate and Citric Acid to
        be prevented from cement contamination. Over treating with Sodium bicarbonate could
        be as detrimental to drilling fluid properties as the cement contamination. Therefore, it is
         not advisable to pretreat with more than 0.5 lb/bbl of Sodium bicarbonate. One approach
         to avoid overtreatment is to treat only soluble calcium and wait to treat lime when it goes
         into solution on subsequent circulations. Treatments should be discontinued when excess
        lime approaches 0.3 to 0.5 lb/bbl.

       Like in the previous sections, also in this section all the precautions have to be considered
         in order to take immediate reactions for any risk of circulation losses: Optimize the
        procedure to fight the losses (see the guide at the end of this mud program) and be ready
        with LCM material to mix LCM pill as quick as possible.

       Good drilling operation is another important parameter to have proper hole condition
          particularly whilst Tripping. Gel strength should be kept at minimum range to minimize
        Surge & Swab pressure.

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
             South Azadegan Oilfield Project

 Contract No:          AZNS-127 Re-Entry#01 Program               Page 103 of 229CCA/C/1401/27

       The mud weight in this interval is critical for successful drilling, hole condition will dictate
         the density. Mud weight will be increased by adding Barite.

        If tight hole or packing off problems is encountered increase the frequency and the volume
         of sweep.

       PH in active system should be maintained between 9.5 – 10

       Good drilling operation is another important parameter to have proper hole condition
          particularly whilst Tripping. Gel strength should be kept at minimum range to minimize
        Surge & Swab pressure.

        It is mandatory to monitor closely the rheological properties in order to ensure adequate
         hole cleaning, run Hydraulic calculation to ensure that mud carrying capacity is enough
         to meet this critical goal.

       Mud can be treated with additional shale inhibitor if deemed necessary to control shale
          reactivity and washouts.

       Optimize the procedure to fight the losses (see the guide at the end of this mud program)
        and be ready with adequate LCM material to mix LCM pill as quick as possible.

       The previouse mud in hole must be dump , due to have enoght surface volume , keep 300
         bbl heavy mud in mud pits as kill mud.

       The concentration of KCl in the system is insufficient; shale cuttings will be soft and
        mushy. If the concentration of KCl is sufficient, they will retain their integrity. As drilling
       commences the potassium ion becomes depleted and must be replenished. The rate of
         depletion provides an indication of how reactive the shales are. While drilling gumbo, the
        appearance of soft mushy cuttings at surface is a good indication that the potassium
         concentration should be increased. Other indicators include a drilled solids increase, a
      CEC increase and the occurrence of tight hole or mud rings.

       Since this system is very solids-sensitive, it is best to mix the system from scratch instead
         of converting an existing drilling fluid (containing drill solids) to a potassium chloride
         system.

       This system is very sensitive to solids and hardness contamination so the first step in
        mixing this system is to treat the hardness.

       Due to necessary of directional drilling, it is recommended to pump (at the first 20 bbl
      Low Vis pill follow by 20 bbl Hi Vis Pill should be pumped every stand) for clean the
         hole. For better understanding of hole cleaning procedure based on hole angels please
          refer to technical appendix.

  Interval Description

 The 8 1/2” “section will be drilled from 3337 m to an estimated section TD of 3939 m with Sal
 KCL Polymer Mud system necessitating a minimum requirement of 80 pcf to 85 pcf based on
 nearby well data.

  It is necessary to adjust mud weight and mud properties as hole condition dictate. Some tight hole,
  well flow and low ROP maybe experienced in this hole section. The drilling fluid engineer will
  advise the drilling supervisor of any extended circulation or sweep requirement. It uses clarified
  viscosifier to provide viscosity and carrying capacity and starch to reduce mud filtrate.

        8.2.1.1  Interval Mud Discussion

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
             South Azadegan Oilfield Project

 Contract No:          AZNS-127 Re-Entry#01 Program               Page 105 of 229CCA/C/1401/27

  Sal KCL Polymer Mud are used to prevent excessive hole enlargement while drilling massive salt
  beds. They can also be used to reduce dispersion and hydration of shales and clays. Solids content
  (percent by volume) should be maintained within a desired range by mechanical removal or
  dilution with saturated salt water to prevent unacceptable increases in gel strengths and viscosity.

 The chloride content of saturated salt muds is 170,000- 188,000 mg/l at saturation. As the
  temperature of the mud increases, more salt is able to go into solution. This means that a fluid
 which is saturated under surface conditions may not be saturated in down hole temperatures and
 can cause substantial washout in a salt zone due to increased salt solubility.

  Control of the pH of saturated salt muds varies widely. Saturated salt muds require larger
  additions of caustic soda to maintain a pH in Salt saturate muds. Maintaining an±9.5 pH offers
  several advantages:

       Deflocculants are more effective

       Corrosion is reduced

       Lower concentrations of filtration control additives are required when Ca++ and Mg++
          solubility is reduced

       Foaming tendency is lessened

       Mud is generally more stable

 Caco3 Polymer Mud will normally contain soluble calcium due to the formations penetrated and
  the type of makeup water used. Also, the sodium ions from the salt will undergo a Base Exchange
 and release calcium ions from the clay platelets, thereby contributing to the free calcium.
  Generally, the presence of calcium does not produce detrimental effects on the mud; except when
  the pH is increased beyond 12.0 and then the fluid loss will be difficult to control.

 Foaming is generally restricted to surface foam and is not troublesome unless aggravated by
 mechanical agitation. The degree of foaming may sometimes be decreased by increasing the
  alkalinity of the mud (Pm). A Defoaming agent may be necessary. Unless Mg++ sensitive
  additives are used, a Saturated Salt Mud is less prone to foam and air cut with a 9.5 - 10 pH. The
  temperature limitation of Saturated Salt Muds is around 250°F and is normally dictated by the
  filtration control additive used. Ca++ and Mg++ hardness do not adversely affect filtration control
  in Saturated Salt Muds when using starch; however.

  Usually there are no problems associated with saturated salt mud conversions. Conversion can be
 accomplished in two circulations. When a freshwater mud is to be converted to a saturated salt
 mud, it is desirable to use as much of the old mud as possible.

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
             South Azadegan Oilfield Project

 Contract No:          AZNS-127 Re-Entry#01 Program               Page 106 of 229CCA/C/1401/27

 The amount of salt required to saturate the system should be calculated and then added through
  the hopper. A saturated salt mud will generally weight 75 pcf without weight material addition.
  Next, a fluid loss additive is added through the hopper while the mud is circulated. Starch is
  generally used for fluid loss control and as a supplementary viscosifier. Normally, 12 lb/bbl starch
  will be required to obtain a fluid loss of less than 3 cc/30 minutes. If a higher density is required,
  Barite which will beuse. When weighting up with Barite, a Lignosulphonate Deflocculant is
 recommended to avoid excessive viscosity and high gel strengths as contingency.

 Potassium is used in areas where inhibition is required to limit chemical alteration of shales.
 Potassium performance is based on cationic exchange of potassium for sodium or calcium ions
 on smectites and interlayered clays. The potassium ion compared to calcium ion or other inhibitive
  ions, fits more closely into the clay lattice structure, thereby greatly reducing hydration of clays.
  Potassium-based muds perform best on shales containing large quantities of smectites or
  interlayered clays in the total clay fraction. Potassium interactions with clay surfaces can be traced
  to two effects: ionic size and hydrational energy. Potassium ions are of the proper size to fit snugly
  into the spaces between the two silica tetrahedral layers which contact each other in the formation
  of a three-layer clay

  packet. The ionic diameter of potassium is 2.66 Å, very close to the available distance of 2.8 Å in
  the lattice space of the clay structure. A cation slightly smaller than 2.8 Å is desirable to allow for
  crystalline compaction.

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
             South Azadegan Oilfield Project

 Contract No:          AZNS-127 Re-Entry#01 Program               Page 107 of 229CCA/C/1401/27

 Product Concentration & Function

                                     Salt Saturated/ Polymer mud

       Product N.                   Ppb                 Product Function
        Caustic Soda                        0.5 - 1                   Alkalinity Control
       Soda Ash                         1                   Hardness Remover
        Salt                      Up to 95           non - solid weighting agent
     KCL                              3.5 – 10.7                  Shale Inhibitor
       Limestone Powder            Up to 114                 weighting agent
        Starch LV                         10-12                  Fluid loss controller
       Xanthan gum                        0.5 - 1                        Viscosifier
       Biocide                   As Requirement                 Anti-bacteria
       Glycol                            Optional                  Shale Inhibitor

                         MIXING PROCEDURE

         1. PH , chloride , calcium and total hardness of drill water ( that water which will be used
        for mud making ) must be check by Mud engineer

         2. Fill the mud tank with Drill water
         3. add Salt & KCL to reach mentioned mud weight
         4. Remove Hardness by add Soda ash
         5. adjust PH & alkalinity by Lime and Caustic Soda
         6. add Starch LV to control fluid loss
         7. add Xanthan gum as Need to reach good rheology
         8. Add limestone powder to reach Mentioned Weight
       9.Check the pH and keep in range by add Lime
        10. Mix Biocide as need

────────────────────────────────────────────
             South Azadegan Oilfield Project

 Challenges

       Possible HT environment

 Recommendation

       Hole cleaning is highly related to low shear rate parameters, so an adequate low shear rate
         yield point should have maintained to ensure hole cleaning. Run Hydraulic calculation to
        ensure that mud carrying capacity is enough to meet this critical goal.

       The previous mud will be transfer to this section.

      A suitable LCM material for reservoir should be available on location to combat down
         hole losses if occurred (see the guide at the end of this mud program).

       Since this system is very solids-sensitive, it is best to mix the system from scratch instead
         of converting an existing drilling fluid (containing drill solids) to a potassium chloride
         system.

       This system is very sensitive to solids and hardness contamination so the first step in
        mixing this system is to treat the hardness.

       Due to necessity of directional drilling, it is recommended to pump (at the first 20 bbl Low
        Vis pill follow by 20 bbl Hi Vis Pill should be pumped every stand) for clean the hole.
        For better understanding of hole cleaning procedure based on hole angels please refer to
         technical appendix.

       Due to face with torque and drag  , it is recommended to mix WATER BASE MUD
         Lubricant 1 – 3% vol reduce torque and drag while drilling horizontal or vertical

        In the directional drilling section it is recommended to use Glass beads 2 – 3 ppb as due
         to have torque and drag.

       The mud weight in this interval is critical for successful drilling, hole condition will dictate
         the density. Mud weight will be increased by adding CaCO3 F.
